# DONE — the archive

Finished tasks, newest at the top. `./kit plan tick <ID>` moves a line here from `plan/TODO.md`, with its
text intact, so a question like "when did the FPU model land, and what did it not cover?" is one `git log`
away. Nothing is ever edited by hand here either; `./kit plan <ID>` finds a task whichever file it is in.

### Next (in this order)

- [x] **P47** the shell runs at about 1 fps (user, 2026-10-20): far below anything a software rasteriser at this resolution explains, so something specific is wrong. Find out why before fixing anything. Profile a real ride (`perf record -g` on the release binary, or `cargo flamegraph`; one player, then four) and time every stage of a frame: sim, animation, skinning, scene build, raster, present, input polling, menu, HUD, plus any file or disc read, decode, allocation or log write that happens per frame. Suspects to rule in or out: work per frame that should happen once (V25's texture resolve or decode, P37's repaint, world or terrain rebuilds, PSM/PST parsing, settings or controls file reads), a debug or opt-level=1 build being run (Cargo.toml's [profile.dev] is opt-level 1; check what `./kit` and the docs launch), a vsync or present that blocks, pad or device polling that blocks (P35's device scan, fifo reads), and logging. Fix every cause you find, and say in the journal what each one cost before and after, measured on the same run. Leave the GPU renderer to P44, which is running at the same time. If the software rasteriser itself turns out to be the cost, make it fast enough to use (threads, culling, no per-pixel allocation) and tell P44 through the journal. The simulation must not move a bit → crates/openmx/src/main.rs · crates/game-render/src/world.rs · crates/game-render/src/raster.rs | t: crates/openmx/tests — a headless ride of N frames at the shell's default size finishes within a time budget in release, and a test that no per-frame stage reads a file or decodes a texture after the first frame | j: one-fps

- [x] **P53** the controller cannot pause the ride or work the pause menu, and on the splash (P46) Cross (the A button on an Xbox-style pad) or Start sometimes does nothing, so the user cannot get into the game at all (user, 2026-10-20). Reproduce it with real and virtual pads (uinput works again) at the user's settings (4K, the GPU renderer), not only in the headless tests. Then find each cause. Suspects: - presses read once per drawn frame as held state rather than as events, so a press and release inside one slow frame (P52, V30: about 5 fps at 4K) is lost; - the menu's reader only hearing 1P's device, when the pad that pressed was given another seat, or the keyboard and an idle or virtual pad (Steam's, another slot's js0) took 1P first; - the pause opened by `pause_for(seat)` hearing only that seat, when the seat number and the device id do not match (menu.rs `pause_for`, main.rs's "any player's Start opens the pause" block); - the Xbox layout's button mapping (gilrs South/East against Cross/Circle), and Start as the Xbox Menu button; - the ride's pad reader eating Start before the front end sees it. Fix them so that any press on the device that owns the screen registers, however slow the frame: queue the device's button events between frames and hand them to the menu, the splash and the pause. Show 1P's device on the splash so a wrong 1P is visible, and let a press on the splash take 1P from a device that has not pressed anything → crates/openmx/src/pad.rs · crates/openmx/src/menu.rs · crates/openmx/src/main.rs | t: crates/openmx/tests/menu.rs — a press and release between two frames still opens the pause and moves its cursor; a seated pad's Start opens the pause and its d-pad and Cross work it; on the splash a pad's Cross makes it 1P even while an idle virtual pad is plugged in, at any frame rate | j: pad-in-the-menus — user, 2026-10-20: the failing pad is a Nintendo Switch Pro Controller on another Linux machine (not this one). Likely causes to check first: (1) hid-nintendo reports buttons by position (A, the right face button, is BTN_EAST; B, the bottom, is BTN_SOUTH), so the shell's DualShock-order PAD_BUTTONS reads Nintendo A as Circle (back) and B as Cross. Treat a Nintendo pad (by name or VID 057e) the Nintendo way by default: A confirms and B goes back, with a setting to use positions instead. (2) hid-nintendo also makes a second js device, 'Nintendo Switch Pro Controller IMU', whose accelerometer axes never rest. Skip it like a virtual pad, or it takes 1P or reads as a drifting stick. (3) Without joycond, or over Bluetooth before pairing finishes, the pad may appear twice or come and go. (4) + is Start and - is Select; check the js order puts them where PAD_BUTTONS expects. Test with a recorded js event stream from a Pro Controller (hid-nintendo's button and axis map) as a fixture, since there is no Pro Controller on this machine. Also check how 8BitDo or Xbox pads come through, so this is not fixed for one pad only

- [x] **P52** the ride goes into slow motion when the picture is slow (user, 2026-10-20: at 4K the bike snaps around, will not grip, and the engine revs on the gas but the bike barely moves). Cause: the shell steps the simulation once per drawn frame, feeding it the wall clock (`wall(started)` into `engine.step`, crates/openmx/src/main.rs). The original's dt pipeline (game-sim tick.rs `Dt::update`: 30-frame average, capped at 1/30 s) then gives every tick at most 1/30 s. At 5 fps drawn, the world runs at a sixth of real time, while audio runs on real time. The uneven frame times also feed the average's ring, so dt jumps about. Decouple them: run the simulation on its own fixed 30 Hz schedule, as the original runs on a PS2 that holds 30 fps, and step as many ticks per drawn frame as the wall clock owes. Feed each tick the clock a steady 30 fps PS2 would have read, not the host's: find the original's real per-frame deltas from the captures (context/capture dt and the timer columns) so dt is the original's exact value. Cap the catch-up so a long stall does not spiral (the original's LONG_FRAME rule may already say what), and draw as fast as the machine can, well above 60 fps (the monitor's refresh with vsync, uncapped without; user, 2026-10-20: "game logic should stay original but we can see high framerates"), every drawn frame interpolating the bike, rider, camera and world between the last two 30 Hz ticks by the time since the last tick, render-side only, so motion is smooth at 120 or 144 Hz and the simulation never sees it. Input is sampled per tick, not per drawn frame. Replays, tapes, online lockstep (N3) and acceptance stay bit for bit. Check whether S55's landing snap was this all along, and say so in its journal → crates/openmx/src/main.rs · crates/openmx/src/stages.rs | t: crates/openmx tests — a headless ride drawn at a forced 5 fps covers the same distance in the same wall time as one at 60 fps and lands on exactly the same simulation state; dt is the same every tick | j: fixed-sim-rate

Measure the frame time against P47's and V30's numbers with the whole map in view at 4K, and write before and after in the journal. - [x] **P88** session markers on the d-pad: save a spot and teleport back to it, to line up a trick and keep trying it (user, 2026-10-21). In a freeride each player has 5 marker slots for the session. Down on the d-pad held 250 ms places a marker in the current slot at the rider's spot (position, facing, and the bike's state there — speed included, so a run-up repeats; say in the journal if standing still turned out better); Up held 250 ms teleports to the current slot's marker; Left and Right step through the 5 slots and then through the other players, so Up on a player teleports you to them. A tap shorter than 250 ms does nothing (so a brush of the d-pad never fires), and each hold fires once until released. A teleport puts you down as a put-back does, ghosted (P57's poof: half see-through, no collision), so you never collide with the player you land on. Show the slot and whether it holds a marker on the HUD briefly when it changes, and draw placed markers faintly in the world for their owner. First check what the original binds to the d-pad in a ride (P73's table) and keep anything it needs. Markers and teleports are pad input on agreed ticks, so they work online under rollback; they live for the session only → crates/openmx/src/main.rs · crates/openmx/src/vehicle.rs · crates/openmx/src/hud.rs · crates/game-sim/src/ride.rs | t: crates/openmx tests — Down held 250 ms places a marker, a 200 ms tap does not, Up held 250 ms lands the rider on it ghosted, Left/Right reach all five slots and then the other players, and a teleport onto another rider collides with nothing | j: session-markers — user, 2026-10-21: the other players are part of the marker UI: stepping Left/Right past your 5 slots shows each player by their name (or P1, P2… when they have none) in the same slot display, and holding Up 250 ms on one teleports you to them, ghosted as above; the UI shows the selection while you step and while you hold

- [x] **MD16** MX vs. ATV's tricks, native (user, 2026-10-23): every trick MX vs. ATV Unleashed and Untamed add over MX Unleashed rides in openmx, and R1 gives that game's whips, 360s and front flips. Source: openmx-mods' notes/tricks.md and its trick packs (its M19: stunt tables, gestures, animations, and how each game drives the whip, the flat spin and the front flip), read from mods/tricks/<game>/ like the track mods (MD2). Controls, new scheme only (P40): Cross and Square are free now that R2/L2 are the throttle and brake, so they become trick buttons for the new tricks, extending trick.rs's gesture (buttons x stick direction) past its 24 slots; R1 stays what it is in the original, the trick modifier, and the air moves are added to it: only while R1 is held does the stick give the new air moves (user, 2026-10-23). From the discs (openmx-mods M19, notes/tricks.md): the 360 left/right is R1 + a stick flick sideways on a lip, its yaw and pitch torque from the .phy's [Three Sixty] scaled by the flick, its 0.9 s grace, its 35-degree clip rule and a mid-clip landing wrecking, as SLUS_211.04 has it; the whip is the air control's lean with MX vs. ATV's weaker auto-level, nothing to add beyond that. The front flip is NEW, in neither game (user, 2026-10-23: make it): R1 + stick forward on a lip, built as the mirror of the port's backflip — the same machinery, clocks and landing test with the pitch turned forward, its torque and rate set so a full turn fits the air a backflip of the same jump has, and its own rows in the trick table (name Frontflip, scored like the backflip); its rider pose is the backflip's clip played for a forward turn until a real one exists, said in the journal and the Settings help. It is the one move here that is not 1:1 with a disc, so it lives only under Tricks MX vs. ATV, and its numbers are named constants with a comment saying they are the port's own. Without R1 the stick does exactly what it does now: pulling back and pushing forward to pump the preload for a backflip, or leaning in the air, must never start a front flip or a 360 (a test rides a backflip's pump with R1 up and asserts no new move starts). Every original R1 trick stays as it is; where an R1 gesture and a new air move would read the same input, the original's wins and the journal says how they are told apart. A setting: Tricks Original (MXU's alone, the default for replays and acceptance) or MX vs. ATV; with Original every replay hash is unchanged, and online all seats must agree (MD6's check). Animations are the source disc's; a missing one is named, not approximated (after MD2; waits on openmx-mods M19) → crates/game-sim/src/trick.rs · crates/game-sim/src/air.rs · crates/openmx/src/pad.rs · crates/openmx/src/menu.rs | t: crates/game-sim tests — a generated trick pack's stunts start from the new gestures; the R1 moves turn the bike in the air and land by their test; Original's replay hashes unchanged | j: mxvatv-tricks — openmx-mods M19 landed (notes/tricks.md, trick packs in out/mods/tricks/)

It shows no LANDED or BAILED word: it turns green when the chain lands and red when it bails, but the movies hold no second colour, so first check what the original does in PCSX2 (land and bail a trick, read the popup's colour word in RAM or a shot) and use that, grey or a fade included. The radar is the original's dark disc with thin axes, N/E/S/W outside the rim, turning with heading, zone letters in their colours, AI as red dots and the player as the yellow arrow, plus P88's markers in their slot colours. Prompts (the machine switch's Would you like to switch to the Biplane? with a black Circle Accept pill, a challenge's Hold to start with a ring) go low and small at the bottom centre, out of the way. The marker menu replaces hud::draw_markers: a dark panel at the top right with the five slot discs in MARKER_COLOURS, the other players' pills, the lit item's label, the hold bar for Down (place) and Up (ride to), and a d-pad legend, shown on the same MarkerBanner rules and per machine (P107). A camera change shows a small toast with the preset's name. - [x] **MD17** a world (MD8) starts in seconds and holds only what is near (user, 2026-10-23: 'we might need some lazyloading optimization'). Today the sim's tiles already load on first use (Tiled::ensure, Tiled::stream), but before the first frame worlds::chunks reads every piece's terrain again, builds its Ground and meshes every chunk, cuts each cell by Tiled::owner, and the renderer uploads all of it: world_mxu_free (5 tiles, 50 joins, 79 MB) makes 4160 chunks in 39 s in release; world_mxu is 27 tiles and 626 joins (696 MB), world_ultra 77 tiles and 2135 joins (2.2 GB). Measure the split first (--timings, or Instant prints per step: read, parse, Ground::new, chunks, owner cut, upload) and put it in the journal. Then build and upload before the ride only the pieces within the spawn's Reach (DESKTOP), and the rest on a background thread nearest first as the rider moves, dropped again past the reach with a margin so a piece at the edge does not reload every frame; this is MD15's 'ground chunks loaded and dropped by distance', so do it here and leave MD15 the props, eco, traffic and seams. Whatever the hot step turns out to be gets its fix: the owner cut done once per piece's rectangle rather than per cell where a piece has no neighbour inside it, a piece's terrain read once and shared by the sim's tile and the ground, the meshing off the window's thread. - [x] **V59** the bike's wheels look wrong in the air; make them look better (user, 2026-10-23). Presentation only, need not be 1:1; the original's look stays one setting away (a sub-switch of P126's Original mode) only if that is free. First see what it is and name it in the journal: film a jump in the shell (./kit shot frames through a jump, or a --play tape off free06's first lip, memory free06-first-jump-is-a-flip-lip) and the same jump in the original (S109's animandpump capture has jumps). Suspects: (a) strobing. In the air the rear wheel spins up freely on the gas, and vehicle.rs's Roll::step turns it by rate x dt each frame. Past half a turn a frame the drawn wheel stands still, turns backwards or jitters (the wagon-wheel effect). Fix by a spin blur past a rate: a blurred tyre and spokes, or a cross-fade to a blurred disc as the biplane's propconeblur does, if the bike's .psg has none; and cap the drawn turn per frame below the alias. (b) the hang. At full droop the wheel drops off the fork or swingarm, or goes through the fender: the shocks' extension, the TireFront/TireRear node turns (vehicle.rs's table), and the airborne row (vehicle_anim::AIR, vehicle.rs:491). Check against the original's droop in the air. - [x] **MD25** a plain 360 (MX vs. ATV's flat spin, MD16) cannot be done: the user tries one and gets a backflip, or a 360 with a backflip in it (user, 2026-10-23: 'i cant do a non-backflip 360'). Reproduce it first: R1 held, the stick flicked hard left or right on a lip, under Tricks MX vs. ATV and the new layout, with the trick packs in mods/tricks (shell and sim). Then find which of these it is and say so in the journal. (a) The 360's own pitch share: air::three_sixty (crates/game-sim/src/air.rs:1242) fires with pitch = -map(min_torque_x, max_torque_x) from the pack's [Three Sixty]. Check those numbers against openmx-mods' notes/tricks.md and SLUS_211.04: whether the sign, the scale or the lane is wrong, so the bike pitches over back. (b) The flip row winning the lip: unleashed_rotate runs before three_sixty in ride.rs:1747, and three_sixty refuses while flip_torque_applied or flip_active. So a stick that passes through back on its way to a side (the pump, the preload) arms the backflip first. (c) The 360 never arming: the arm needs step.modifier and |x| > SPIN_HELD on a lip, then fires the next frame. If on_a_lip is false by then, or side_flick is 0, the spin does nothing and only the backflip shows. Fix it to MX vs. ATV's: a sideways flick with R1 on a lip gives a flat 360 with only the pitch the disc gives it, and MD20's diagonal is the one way to get a 360 backflip. MX Unleashed's backflip under Tricks Original stays bit for bit → crates/game-sim/src/air.rs · crates/game-sim/src/ride.rs | t: crates/game-sim tests: R1 plus a full sideways flick off a generated lip turns about 360 degrees of yaw with the pitch under a named bound and lands clean, both ways; a stick that sweeps back first still gives the 360, if MX vs. ATV does; Original's replay hashes unchanged | j: plain-360 — user, 2026-10-23, at the top: a 360 cannot be stopped; it keeps turning until the rider bails. Lead: air::three_sixty's slowing (air.rs:1293) only runs with R1 let go and the stick away from the spin's side, and it is scaled by |x|. So with the stick centred the torque never decays, the bike turns on, and landing part way round (the 35-degree clip rule) wrecks. Find from SLUS_211.04 how MX vs. ATV ends a 360: whether letting go of the stick or R1 slows it, whether it stops by itself at one turn (the clip coming back inside 35 degrees), and how it lands clean; match it. Test: a 360 let go after the flick comes round once and lands clean, as MX vs. ATV's does — found 2026-10-23 (openmx-mods notes/tricks.md, the 360's substep, matched by air::three_sixty): MX vs. ATV's 360 is a two-way gesture on the lip, not a sideways push. With R1 alone held, the stick past 0.7 to one side arms it and then past 0.7 to the other side fires it (left then right spins one way, right then left the other); a lip is a wheel down, the body rising faster than 5, speed over 30 and no brake, a few frames, and leaving the lip disarms. A plain push to one side never fires, so the player gets the backflip's pump alone. Its pitch torque (600 to 1100, against 200 to 1100 of yaw) tips the nose up by design, and its only slowing is R1 let go and the stick pushed against the spin; with the stick idle it turns on, so stopping without a bail is the original's too. So the port is likely faithful; check that, then report whether MXU's bike inertia makes the nose-up worse than MX vs. ATV's (same flick in both). Asked the user whether they want an easier 360 of the port's own, as a switch; until they answer, change nothing beyond a fault found — user's answer, 2026-10-23: an easy 360 of the port's own, on by default. With R1 held, one sideways push past 0.7 on the lip fires a flat 360 that way (no arm-then-cross), with no nose-up pitch torque, and it eases to a stop after one full turn (the yaw torque taken off as the heading comes back inside 35 degrees of take-off, or a named decay) so it lands clean with no stick work. A switch, 360 style: Easy (default) or MX vs. ATV's exact step; it goes under P126's Original mode (Original sets the exact one). The easy path's rules are named constants with a comment saying they are the port's own. Tests: Easy, one push right on a generated lip turns about 360 degrees flat, comes to rest within 35 degrees and lands clean; the same left; MX vs. ATV's style still needs the two-way gesture and keeps its nose-up, its replay unchanged; MD20's diagonal 360 flips still work under both
- [x] **P141** a teleport onto another player puts you where they are, in the air too, not on the ground under them (user, 2026-10-23: 'not teleport to ground when teleporting to other players'). Two places drop to the ground today: Garage::spot (vehicle.rs, P104) hands a player in the plane as the ground under the plane, upright and stopped, so a bike ridden to a flying player stands below them; and markers::fly's Up on a player lands the plane on the ground at the player, at rest (vehicle.rs land, Garage::markers and lane_markers alike, refused with no ground there). Instead: the spot is the player's machine as it is that tick, its position, attitude and motion (P111's Spot motion for a bike, the Flight for a plane). Plane onto a flying plane: fly on as theirs flies (a Flight put_back of their flight's motion onto this seat's plane, its own livery, throttle and wreck state cleared), a little behind or beside so the two do not overlap (state the offset). Plane onto a rider: in the air at their spot plus a clearance, at their velocity, flying, with the stall it brings if slow (a rider at rest gets the ground landing it has today, the one case left). Bike onto a flying plane: at the plane's spot less a clearance under it, with the plane's velocity, airborne, and falls as a jump would (no wreck for the teleport itself: the same unwreck and grace a marker's put-back gets). Bike onto a bike stays P111's ride_as, which already keeps the air. - [x] **P137** game modifiers: a Biplane max G knob, since a sped-up biplane turns far too fast (user, 2026-10-23: 'the sped up biplanes turn super fast'). Lift and the surfaces' moments go with airspeed squared, so at 2x top speed the same stick pulls ~4x the g, twice the turn rate and a much sharper roll; the autopilot's MaxPitchRate loop does not help because any aileron outside its dead zone sends the stick straight to the elevator (biplane::autopilot), and P108's Biplane turn speed scales every speed alike (taming the top end makes it mushy slow). One knob, per seat beside the other Biplane rows: Off (the original, bit for bit, the default under both layouts) or 3 g to 9 g. On, each step clamp the elevator command (open-loop and the PI's alike) to the deflection the current speed and angle of attack allow before the wing's normal load passes the limit, and ease the ailerons by (corner speed / speed)^2 above the corner speed the limit implies, so below about the stock top speed nothing changes and above it a full pull holds the set g (turn radius growing with speed) and the roll does not flick. Carried by Mods like the other knobs: settings/presets/tapes, Mods::replane and P98's retune in flight, the session markers' put_back → crates/game-sim/src/modifiers.rs · crates/game-sim/src/biplane.rs · crates/game-sim/src/flight.rs · crates/openmx/src/menu.rs | t: crates/game-sim/tests — at 300% top speed a full pull from level cruise never exceeds the set g on any step and a full aileron roll rate stays near the stock top speed's; at stock speed and settings the limiter never acts (bit-equal flight with the knob On at 9 g over a stock cruise); Off flies the original's numbers bit for bit; a marker and a retune in flight keep the knob | j: biplane-max-g
- [x] **P138** an Air Wrecks switch on Settings' Ride tab beside Wrecks: Off, the rider is never thrown off for the bike's attitude in the air — upside down, nosed over, rolled past the limit — and rides the jump out (user, 2026-10-23: 'a switch in ride to disable bailing inair from going upside down and such'). The in-air bails are air::wreck_test's airborne clause (NoseDive and Tail past AirbornePitchDown/Up/RollWreckAngle while not flipping or spinning); Off skips just that clause, and the landing is still judged as it is (the ground pitch angles, the g loads, the crossup, the trick endings), so landing upside down still wrecks. On (the original, bit for bit) is the default and Original mode's. Carried the way Wrecks is (settings.txt, Sense beside wrecks_enabled, its snapshot wire, presets/tapes, netplay's agreed settings) and taking at the pause's close as the Ride rows do (P98) → crates/game-sim/src/air.rs · crates/game-sim/src/state.rs · crates/game-sim/src/rider.rs · crates/openmx/src/menu_pages.rs | t: crates/game-sim tests: a ride held upside down through a long jump wrecks NoseDive/Tail in the air with the switch On and rides on Off, and Off still wrecks a landing on the roof; On is bit-equal to today over a stock tape; crates/openmx/tests/menu.rs: the row toggles and survives a restart | j: air-wrecks-switch

- [x] **V91** the roads and ruts still look like the PS2's on Bridgeport (free09) with Texture Filtering at 16x (user, 2026-10-26, after V83). Cause, read from the code: the ground reads the road/rut paint targets (gpu/ground_mesh.rs APPLY_WGSL, mesh_near/mesh_far) through clamp_sampler (gpu.rs), which has anisotropy 1 and wgpu's default Nearest mip filter, and is outside V83's sampler cache, so set_anisotropy never touches it: down the road one isotropic mip is picked (smear) and mips switch in hard bands, as the GS draws. V83's journal names this gap. Fix: give the targets' group 2 a sampler from V83's cache (clamped u/v, linear mips, the renderer's anisotropy; a SamplerKey for it) and rebuild every near target's and the far one's bind group in Renderer::set_anisotropy as V83 does for materials and the ground; Off stays plain trilinear for the Original switch. - [x] **MD29** worlds that wrap around (user, 2026-10-23: "on the WORLD maps it just loops and you can wrap around the map so there's no end you'd just end on the other side"). A world's switch (its world file, off by default) that turns its edges into seams: ride off the east edge and come in on the west. The original shoots a rider off the edge back to the middle (off_map.rs), so this is the port's own departure and off_map stays the original's for every disc map and every world without the switch. The sim: when the rider crosses a seam, shift every world-space position (chassis, wheels, rider, ragdoll, camera, particles, put-back record, ghosts) by exactly the world's width, a whole number of feet under 2^24 so the shift is exact as tiles.rs argues; ground and shape queries near a seam take their point modulo the width so a probe reads the far side; Tiled::keeps and the put-back wrap; the biplane's Area too. The worlds: the far edge must meet the near one, so a join terrain from the last tile back to the first on each wrapped axis (generate.rs). The draw: tiles drawn again one width off on each side out to the fog's reach (Far Fog or the original's; Whole Map on a wrapped world reaches the copies' ends only), other riders at their nearest copy, the radar wrapped. Online, every peer shifts on the same tick, so it stays deterministic. After V65 → crates/game-sim/src/tiles.rs · crates/game-sim/src/off_map.rs · crates/openmx/src/worlds.rs · crates/game-render/src/gpu.rs | t: crates/game-sim tests: a ride across a seam is the same ride, bit for bit after the shift, as the same ride on a world built one width wider; the edge throw never fires on a wrapped axis; crates/openmx/tests: a tiled_world-style ride round a wrapped world | j: wrapped-worlds — user, 2026-10-26: moved out of Stretch into the queue. Also: the minimap shows the name of the map (tile) the rider is on, under the radar; and the radar's direction markers name the map that lies in each direction (the neighbouring tile's map, across a seam on a wrapped axis), so a rider can steer for a map. Both in every world, wrapped or not; t: crates/openmx tests — the radar's label is the current tile's map name, changes as the rider crosses a tile edge or a seam, and each direction's label is that neighbour's — user, 2026-10-26: moved up behind V65; the minimap's map names are split out to MD42 — on a wrapped axis, make MD42's direction labels name the map across the seam

### Phase 2 — a decompilation you can read
- [x] **R1** (after D4) Ghidra: `./kit ghidra` on the boot ELF and each overlay, with the EmotionEngine extension, producing `context/decomp/<program>.c`. Check the dumps are at the right addresses (a function in the overlay dump should sit inside the overlay's load range) and note how long a full analysis takes on this machine → kit.toml `[ghidra]` · ghidra_scripts/ | t: `./kit fn <addr>` prints one function from each program | j: r1-decompile
- [x] **R2** (after R1) The PS2 library functions Ghidra cannot name: `printf`/`sprintf`, `memcpy`, `malloc`, the SIF/IOP calls, `scePad*`, `sceGs*`. Name them in `[symbols]` so every later dump reads in terms of them instead of `FUN_…`. Finding `printf` first is worth hours: its call sites are labelled strings that say what the surrounding code is → kit.toml `[symbols]` · research/oracle.txt | t: `./kit fn @printf` resolves | j: r2-symbols
- [x] **R3** (after R1) Find the main loop: the function that runs forever, waits for vsync and calls a handful of per-frame things. Work out its structure — what runs before the sim, what runs after, where rendering is kicked off — and write it down as a numbered tick order. Everything in the port hangs off this order → research/journal/ · docs/CONTEXT.md | t: none (notes) | j: r3-main-loop
- [x] **R4** (after R3) The game's state machine: the mode variable the main loop switches on (title, menu, loading, in-game, replay, results) and the function per mode. Name them in `[symbols]`. This is the map you will navigate by for the rest of the port → kit.toml `[symbols]` | t: `./kit xref @main_loop` lists one handler per mode | j: r3-main-loop
- [x] **R6** (after C3) The actor/entity table: where live objects are kept in RAM (base address, stride, how many), and the first few fields of one — position, velocity, state, type. Dump the same table across a handful of frames from a save state so the stride and the fields are not guesses → research/oracle.txt · docs/CONTEXT.md | t: a dumped table from a state parses with the documented stride | j: r6-actor-table

### Phase 3 — the frame, and your first capture
- [x] **C1** (after D1) Get PCSX2 running the game under the kit: `./kit pcsx2 start`, `./kit pcsx2 status`, `./kit shot` for a screenshot, each on a kit-managed copy with its own config. Confirm `KIT_PCSX2=2` gives a second, independent copy → kit.toml `[emu]` · docs/PCSX2.md | t: `./kit shot` writes a non-black PNG of the title screen | j: c1-emulator
- [x] **C3** (after C1) Save states for the places you will keep coming back to (a human makes these: see `plan/HUMAN.md`) — the title screen, the first in-game frame, mid-action — and a note of what each one holds. Document which slots are the human's (never overwritten) and which are scratch → plan/HUMAN.md · plan/REFERENCE.md | t: none | j: c3-save-states
- [x] **C4** (after C3) First recording: `./kit record` with a watch list (a few addresses from R6) over a few hundred frames from a save state, to a CSV in `context/captures/`. Check it yourself for the failure that costs the most time — a short or stalled capture that looks fine — and confirm the tool shouts `CAPTURE FAILED` when you kill the emulator mid-run → ps2kit/record.py (read only) · context/captures/ | t: the CSV has one row per frame with no vsync gaps | j: c4-first-capture
- [x] **C2** (after C1) The vsync counter: `./kit probe vsync` finds a word in RAM that goes up by one per frame; confirm it stalls when the game pauses and put it in `[emu] vsync_addr`. Every capture and every timing comparison is counted in this one number → kit.toml `[emu]` | t: `./kit pine watch <addr>` shows +1 per frame for 300 frames | j: c1-emulator
- [x] **C5** (after C1) The pad: `./kit pad` presses a button on the running game and the game reacts. Then find where the game reads the pad in RAM (the bitmask per pad, and whether it keeps "pressed this frame" separately) → kit.toml `[symbols]` · docs/PCSX2.md | t: a scripted button press changes the menu selection in a capture | j: c5-pad

### Phase 4 — the arithmetic, before any ported math
- [x] **F1** The EE FPU in `crates/ps2-float`: add, sub, mul, div, sqrt with the PS2's rules — round toward zero, no denormals (flushed), no NaN or infinity (clamped to the largest magnitude), and the smaller addend masked to the exponent difference minus one guard bit. Port physics before this and you will not know which of the two is wrong → crates/ps2-float/src/fpu.rs | t: crates/ps2-float/tests/fpu.rs | j: f1-ee-fpu
- [x] **R5** (after R1) The RNG: find it (look for a 32-bit multiply by a constant and a stored seed, cross-referenced from everywhere), port it, and prove it with the oracle over 10k calls from a dumped seed. A wrong RNG makes every later proof noisy, and it is a 20-line function → crates/game-sim/src/rng.rs · research/oracle.txt | t: crates/game-sim/tests/rng.rs | j: r5-rng
- [x] **F2** `madd.s`/`msub.s` and the accumulator: the product is truncated before the add, so a multiply-add is *not* a multiply then an add. Most of a PS2 game's vector math goes through these, so a wrong one shows up as a tiny drift that takes days to find → crates/ps2-float/src/fpu.rs | t: crates/ps2-float/tests/fpu.rs | j: f1-ee-fpu
- [x] **F4** (after O1) The game's own transcendentals: whatever `sinf`, `cosf`, `atan2f`, `sqrtf` and `powf` the disc links. They are rarely the standard library's, and a port that calls the host's `sin` will differ in the last bits of every angle. Port the disc's implementations and check them against the oracle over the full input range → crates/ps2-float/src/libm.rs (new) · research/oracle.txt | t: crates/ps2-float/tests/libm.rs | j: f4-libm
- [x] **F5** (after F3) The matrix and quaternion helpers the game uses for bones and cameras, built on F1–F3 with the same op order as the original (the order changes the last bits) → crates/game-sim/src/math.rs | t: crates/game-sim/tests/math.rs | j: f5-matrix

### Phase 5 — the first proven function, and the harness
- [x] **A1** (after F1) The state hash: one function that walks the whole simulation state and hashes it, per tick, with a dump mode that says which field first differed. On an almost empty simulation this feels pointless; it is the highest-leverage task in the project, because from here on every port either keeps the hashes matching or does not, and you find out in seconds → crates/game-sim/src/hash.rs | t: crates/game-sim/tests/hash.rs | j: a1-state-hash
- [x] **A2** (after A1) The replay harness: a fixture of recorded inputs plus the initial state, run through the simulation, compared tick by tick against the captured hashes or watch values. Make a one-line failure (`tick 413: player.vel.x 1.5 vs 1.500001`), not a diff of two files → crates/game-sim/src/replay.rs · crates/game-sim/tests/replay.rs | t: crates/game-sim/tests/replay.rs | j: a2-replay-harness
- [x] **O1** (after R1) The oracle, against one trivial function: `./kit oracle` interprets the real code from a RAM dump and calls it with your arguments. Prove it on something with an obvious answer first (a clamp, a distance, a table lookup) so the interpreter is a tool rather than a suspect. Add any unknown opcode it panics on → crates/ps2-oracle/src/ · research/oracle.txt | t: crates/ps2-oracle/tests/basics.rs | j: o1-oracle
- [x] **A3** (after A2) Make the hash platform-independent and prove it: the same fixture hashes identically on x86-64 and on aarch64 (and on wasm32 if this port will run in a browser). Transcendentals go through the game's own `libm`, never the host's → crates/game-sim/tests/platforms.rs | t: crates/game-sim/tests/platforms.rs | j: a3-platforms — moved to stretch: a hash that agrees across architectures matters when the port ships, not while one bike is being made to ride
- [x] **O2** (after O1) Oracle coverage for the arithmetic of F1–F4: run each ported op against the real code over ≥10k generated inputs, including the awkward ones (zero, denormals, the largest magnitudes, equal exponents, exponent differences of 24 and over) → crates/ps2-float/tests/ · research/oracle.txt | t: crates/ps2-float/tests/oracle.rs | j: o2-float-oracle

### Phase 6 — input, the game's own way
- [x] **I1** (after C5) The input layer: pads mapped to the game's own bitmask, with "held", "pressed this frame" and "released" exactly as the original keeps them (including the frame it latches them, which decides whether a press on the frame of a state change counts) → crates/game-sim/src/input.rs | t: crates/game-sim/tests/input.rs | j: i1-input
- [x] **I2** (after I1) Analogue sticks: the original's dead zone, clamping and any curve, in its arithmetic. An approximated stick curve quietly changes every movement in the game → crates/game-sim/src/input.rs | t: crates/game-sim/tests/input.rs (oracle) | j: i1-input

### Phase 4 — the arithmetic, before any ported math
- [x] **F3** VU0 macro mode: the ops the game actually uses (`vmul`, `vadd`, `vmadd`, `vmula`, `vdiv`, `vsqrt`, `vrsqrt`, the clipping and min/max ones), with VU0's own rounding and its flag behaviour. Take the list from the disassembly rather than implementing the whole ISA → crates/ps2-float/src/vu.rs | t: crates/ps2-float/tests/vu.rs | j: f3-vu0

### Phase 7 — one subsystem, end to end

Layout: fileVersion u8, emulator char[50], author char[255], game char[255], totalFrames u32, undoCount u32, savestate u8 = 570 bytes of header, then 18 bytes per frame per pad for two pads (130392 bytes = 3622 x 36). ### Phase 9 — it has to feel like the game
- [x] **I5** the shell's keyboard and its stick: a held key flickers, and when it does register it slams. window.rs keeps key state straight off the X11 wire with no auto-repeat filtering, and X delivers a KeyRelease immediately followed by a KeyPress of the same keycode at the same timestamp about thirty times a second while a key is held down — so held() drops false for a frame on every repeat and the sim's pressed/released edges see dozens of taps where a person held one button. Turn repeat off for the window (ChangeKeyboardControl, opcode 102, no reply, which suits this module's no-reply design) or swallow the pair by timestamp. Then the axes: pad::key_axis only ever answers 0, 128 or 255, so the keyboard asks for full deflection on the frame a key goes down and lean, steer and preload are all-or-nothing through I2's dead zone and curve — ramp the two keyboard sticks toward the edge at a rate and let them return to centre. This is the task behind 'i cant tell my keys are doing anything' → crates/openmx/src/window.rs · crates/openmx/src/pad.rs | t: unit tests in both modules (cargo test runs a bin crate's own): an event stream with a repeat pair in it leaves the key held, and a held direction reaches the edge over several frames rather than on the first | j: keyboard-that-registers
- [x] **V9** nothing on the bike moves: the shell places each part of a .sub at its chassis node's bind transform (crates/openmx/src/main.rs, chassis.model.world(i) once per frame off the static model), so the forks never compress although the ground step prints their travel every frame, the wheels never turn although their angular velocity is right there, the front end never steers with the stick and the swingarm never moves. Worse, S25's vehicle animation machine runs every frame and asks for the bike's own clips — nothing consumes the pose it answers, so the one animation caller the original wrote drives nothing on screen. Two halves: pose the chassis skeleton from the vehicle's driver with the same Rig::driven V8 used for the rider, and drive the suspension, wheel and steering nodes from the simulation's own numbers rather than from a clip, which is where those come from in the original. Ridden, this is the difference between a bike and a decal → crates/game-render/src/scene.rs · crates/openmx/src/main.rs | t: crates/game-render/tests/vehicle.rs — a minute of riding poses the chassis every frame with finite transforms, the fork node travels with the ported compression and the wheel node's angle integrates its own spin, and a stunt's first and last frames differ in the bike's pose | j: bike-that-moves
- [x] **P4** the bike will not stand still, and left alone it spins out. Measured, nobody on the pad: ./target/release/openmx --trace 600 on free06 with the 125 rolls off the start line by itself (337.9 feet down to 238.6 in 400 frames, horizontal speed up to 45 units/s with zero throttle), and at the bottom of the hill the roll winds up to 0.47 rad and the heading swings from -13 to +70 degrees in forty frames — the bike carves a circle on its own, which is what the user saw as 'the user is spinning'. Find which upright is missing rather than damping it by eye: S21's four rider control structs (roll, pitch, donut, balance) and whether the ground step reads them, the brake or the clutch holding a bike at rest, held.stick centring, and whether freeride holds the bike until the first throttle at all. The oracle exists — S8's second capture is the original idling on this save state for 1,811 ticks, so what the real bike does when nobody touches it is a file, not a guess → crates/game-sim/src/ride.rs · crates/game-sim/src/move.rs | t: crates/game-sim/tests/ride.rs — a ten-second no-input ride on real .phy parameters stays within a few feet of where it was dropped and keeps roll under a tenth of a radian, and the idle capture's first seconds match | j: bike-stands-still
- [x] **V10** the two things V8 left out of a drawn rider, both of which show at speed: the five LOD groups in a .psg (the body is drawn from group 0 at every distance, so a rider two hundred feet away costs what the one on your bike costs) and whatever the original picks between them; and the inverse kinematics the four dummy anchors exist for — a hand that stays on the bar and a foot that stays on the peg through a whip or a one-hander, which is what makes a stunt read as the rider holding on rather than a clip playing. V8 measured the anchors to a third of an inch in the riding poses, so the rig to solve against is already there and proved → crates/game-render/src/rider.rs · crates/game-render/src/psg.rs | t: crates/game-render/tests/body.rs — the group chosen falls back as the camera pulls away and the triangle count falls with it, and through a gesture that takes an arm off the bars the solved hand stays within an inch of the grip anchor | j: rider-lod-and-ik
- [x] **S27** the rider's own animation state machine, which nothing in the port is: V8 poses the body from the *vehicle's* driver (S20/V7/S25) because that is the only selection the port has, so every clip in a rider's own set that the vehicle has no reason to ask for is dead weight on the disc — get_ready_ (the rider settling onto the bike before the gate), the wreck_ family, the reverse_ clips, and whatever drives a head or a look. Find the body actor's selection in the decompilation and port it as its own module: the two rider clip prefixes and the body actor's spawn are the thread to pull (research/oracle.txt, and the V8 journal entry names exactly how far V8 got), and the disc's rider motions.ini sections say which two modes exist. The test it has to pass is behavioural, not structural: a rider dropped onto a bike plays its get-ready clip and then its idle without the vehicle asking for anything, and a wreck puts the body into a wreck clip rather than leaving it riding → crates/game-sim/src/rider_anim.rs · crates/game-sim/src/rider.rs | t: crates/game-sim/tests/rider_anim.rs — each state of the ported machine asks for the clip the original's does, one state at a time, the way S25's jump table was proved | j: rider-animation
- [x] **P6** you cannot see what the game thinks you pressed: an overlay, behind a flag, drawing the pad as the simulation reads it — the sixteen controls with held and pressed-this-frame marked, both sticks where input.rs's curve put them, and beside them the numbers that say whether the ride is doing what you asked: speed, rpm, gear, the clutch ramp, grounded, roll and pitch, the state machine's state, the last trick and the last wreck reason. It is the shell's own debugging furniture and matches nothing on the disc, so draw it the cheapest way the rasteriser allows. Two reasons it is worth a task: a person riding cannot otherwise tell a dead key from a bike that ignores the key, and every feel task in this phase is reported with it → crates/openmx/src/hud.rs · crates/openmx/src/main.rs | t: none (by eye) — but a frame of it must cost under a millisecond and the flag off must draw nothing | j: riding-overlay
- [x] **P7** a ride you can play back: the shell writes every frame's pad to a tape and rides a tape instead of a pad (--record FILE, --play FILE), on I3's existing PadTape so a tape of the shell and a tape off the original are the same kind of file. What it buys is that a control complaint stops being a story — 'it spins out after ten seconds' becomes a file somebody else can ride, and the physics tasks in this phase get a repeatable input without a human or a controller at the keyboard. The kit's uinput pad (./kit pad) already drives the shell as /dev/input/js0, so this is the recording half, not the injection half → crates/openmx/src/main.rs · crates/game-sim/src/replay.rs | t: crates/game-sim/tests/replay.rs — a tape written by the shell's own writer reads back frame for frame, and a played tape produces the same state hash twice | j: shell-tapes
- [x] **V11** the blends nothing ever produces: V7 ported the animation set's three- and four-clip blends and V8 drew 3,598 one-clip frames and two two-clip frames in a minute of riding, because no arm of the driver's select answers more than two — while S25 found the two writers in the vehicle's own machine that ask for the four-clip blend that exists for nothing else in the image. Either the port's select is missing the arm that gets there or those two writers are not wired to the driver; find out which, and draw one. A blend the original uses and the port never reaches is a pose the port cannot produce, and the way it shows is a transition that snaps where the game's slides → crates/game-sim/src/motion.rs · crates/game-sim/src/vehicle_anim.rs · crates/game-render/src/anim.rs | t: crates/game-sim/tests/motion.rs and crates/game-render/tests/anim.rs — a ride reaches a three- and a four-clip blend through the real machine rather than a handmade call, and the pose it writes is between the clips it names | j: blends-that-never-run
- [x] **S26** the wreck, and getting back up: a ride ends at its first crash today — the shell prints 'wreck: reason N' and L3+R3 is the only way back, because the automatic reset wants a settle window a bike's class never asks for (S5) and the wrecked bike itself is not ported. Port the crash: Wreckbike.phy, which the disc ships beside the bike's own and nothing reads yet, the rider separating from it (the wreck_sep gestures are in the rider's own set), the bike tumbling under its own parameters, and the recovery the game actually uses — how long it lies there, where it puts you back, and what it resets. Reason 5 is the one the human's capture hit twice (ticks 3259-3349 and 4167-4190, 91 and 24 ticks long), so the capture says how long a real recovery takes. The shell's --no-wrecks flag exists to dodge all of this and should stop being the only way to ride for a minute → crates/game-sim/src/ride.rs · crates/game-sim/src/phy.rs · crates/game-sim/src/state.rs | t: crates/game-sim/tests/ride.rs — a wreck at reason 5 comes back riding within the capture's own window without a button press, and the two captured wrecks' lengths match | j: wreck-and-recover

Until it exists, 'the rider looks right' is one person's eye on a screenshot, and so is every animation task above this one. It is also the only way to tell a wrong clip from a right clip played at the wrong phase → crates/game-sim/tests/motion.rs · watch/ · crates/ps2-oracle/src/ | t: crates/game-sim/tests/motion.rs — a captured frame's joints and the port's agree to a tolerance the entry states and defends, with the worst joint named | j: animation-against-the-original

### Phase 10 — drivable
- [x] **V18** the rider is posed by the wrong machine, and it is the reason the drawn body looks wrong: Rider::body_frame - the sixty-state rider machine S27 ported and V13 proved drives the rider's 22-joint skeleton off Rider\\Anim\\ - is called by nothing in the port except its own test (crates/game-sim/tests/rider_anim.rs). The shell poses the body from world.sim.rider.animation instead, which is the *bike's* motion::Driver frame, exactly as V8 wired it before V13 found the two machines were swapped. What that draws: three seconds into a ride on free06 with no input, the bike upright on both wheels (pitch -0.092 rad, roll -0.0007, contact 111, 3.5 mph), the rider stands bolt upright with straight legs, its arms at its sides and its head a clear head above the bars, while first-draw --pose turn_riding_Mid draws the same body crouched over the same bike. crates/game-render/tests/body.rs is green on all eight including the four-anchor test, so the body can be put on the bike - what reaches it in the shell is not a riding pose. Call body_frame from the ride's own step, hand its vehicle_anim::Frame to the renderer's body, and leave the bike's driver posing the bike — crates/openmx/src/main.rs, crates/game-sim/src/rider.rs and crates/game-render/src/rider.rs | t: crates/game-render/tests/body.rs - a frame of the shell's own driving poses the body inside the anchor tolerance the posed-rider test already measures, and not in the bind pose | j: v18-the-pose-the-shell-draws
- [x] **P9** the throttle is a switch, and the original's is not: crates/openmx/src/pad.rs writes a full 0xff pressure byte for every digital control (the comment at the top of the report builder says so outright), so Cross is 0 or 1 and nothing in between, while only L2 and R2 go through trigger_pressure. The DualShock 2's face buttons are pressure-sensitive and the original's binding table reads the pressure byte, which is how the game is ridden on part throttle out of a corner - the one thing a rider does with the throttle more than pinning it. On top of that the 125's own gas_rate_limit is 10.0 a second, so even a stab is full throttle in three frames. Three halves: read a real pad's per-button pressure where the device reports one; give the shell a way to ride part throttle on hardware that has none, by mapping gas onto a trigger axis (which the port already reads as a pressure) and giving the keyboard's throttle the same ramped KeyAxis I5 gave the stick; and measure what the original actually does - count the ticks of the human recording that hold a partial throttle, so the answer is the capture's and not a guess — crates/openmx/src/pad.rs and crates/openmx/src/window.rs | t: crates/openmx/tests - a pressure byte between 1 and 254 reaches Controls::gas as that fraction, and a keyboard throttle ramps rather than steps | j: p9-analog-throttle
- [x] **P10** nothing in the port says the bike can be ridden. Every proof here is per-function or per-replay: the oracle checks one call, the capture checks one recorded minute tick by tick, and both were green through the whole of Phase 9 while full throttle was looping the bike over backwards in six tenths of a second. Add the test that would have caught it - a scripted minute over the shell's own pad (./kit pad, or the Controls the pad produces, so it runs headless in ./kit check): launch from a standstill, accelerate to the bike's top speed, hold a turn each way, brake to a stop, take one jump and land it. Each leg asserts what a rider would notice - that the bike reaches a speed and keeps it, that it turns the way the stick is pushed, that it stops, that it lands on its wheels - and none of them wrecks. The numbers come from the original where the capture has them and by eye where it does not, and the point is the legs, not the tolerances: a drivability test that is loud when the bike stops being drivable — crates/openmx/tests/ride.rs (new) and crates/game-sim/tests/ride.rs | t: the new file itself - it is the test | j: p10-a-minute-you-can-ride
- [x] **P8** full throttle loops the bike out before it moves: from the start line on free06 with gas pinned and nothing else touched, the chassis pitch runs 0.0097 to 1.3111 radians over frames 8-18, the front wheel's load goes 248 to 0 at frame 9 and never comes back, and the ride wrecks (reason 11) at frame 18 - six tenths of a second after the throttle opens, which is why the bike reads as not moving at all. With --no-wrecks it flips instead (roll -2.41 rad by frame 20) and the peak vertical g over 300 frames is 31.6. The wheelie controller is not the cause and can be ruled out first: move::roll_pitch's wheelie needs lean_fore_aft >= WHEELIE_LEAN (0.5) and a centred stick is 0, so that arm never runs. The arithmetic to look at is the drive force's couple: rear surface0's drive coefficient is 2.2 against a rear load of 824 lbf at frame 8, so about 1813 lbf is applied at the contact patch 2.0 ft (mv.cofg.y) under the centre of gravity - 3626 ft.lbf into an inertia.x of 22.4, or 162 rad/s2 - while the bike's own weight about the rear patch (mass 12.42 x gravity 400 x the rear mount's 2.59 ft, about 12870 ft.lbf) should be holding the nose down and is not. That is the same shape as the bug P4 found in the lateral force: a patch force turned into a couple on the chassis without the ground reaction that balances it. Prove the fix against the oracle, then against the human's capture, which has the original's own full-throttle launches in it — crates/game-sim/src/ride.rs and crates/game-sim/src/move.rs | t: crates/game-sim/tests/ride.rs - full throttle held from a standstill on level ground keeps both wheels loaded and the pitch under the wheelie threshold for a second, and the measured forward acceleration matches the original's | j: p8-full-throttle-loops-out — correction to the arithmetic above, which had the bike's weight wrong by a factor of twelve: the .phy's 'gravity 400' is a force in pounds, not an acceleration - the trace's own fall is 1.073 of velocity a frame, which is 32.2 ft/s2, and 400/12.42 is exactly that. So the bike weighs 400 lbf, not 4969, and at rest the two springs carry 200 and 194 of it, which is right. That makes the launch worse rather than better: the rear load of 824 lbf at frame 8 is twice the whole bike's weight, the 2.2 drive coefficient on it is about 1813 lbf of thrust - four and a half g on a 125 that should make about half a g - and the couple it makes about the contact patch (3626 ft.lbf) is three and a half times the weight's own restoring moment about the rear patch (400 lbf x 2.59 ft, 1036 ft.lbf). The bike cannot not wheelie. Start at the drive force's size, not at the pitch it causes
- [x] **P11** the bike steers the wrong way and will not come back up. Traced on free06 with gas at 0.2 and the stick pinned FULL RIGHT for thirteen seconds: the heading rises for the first four seconds (2.9 to 43.8 degrees) and then reverses - frames 200 to 325 it falls steadily, 41.4 to 11.1 degrees, turning LEFT with the stick held right - while the roll sits parked at -0.738 radians (42 degrees of lean) for two hundred frames and never recovers. Full lock is also only about 45 degrees of total heading change in four seconds at 28 ft/s, where a bike on full lock at that speed should be carving a continuous circle. Two defects in one trace, and they may be one: something in the lean-to-steer path has a sign that flips once the lean passes some angle, and nothing rights a bike that is leaned over with no stick asking for it. This is the other half of what makes the port unrideable - P8 is the throttle, this is the stick - and it is also what the spinning was always about. The original is in the capture: the human's minute has long steady turns in it, so the heading rate against lean angle can be compared tick by tick rather than argued about — crates/game-sim/src/ride.rs and crates/game-sim/src/move.rs | t: crates/game-sim/tests/ride.rs - a held stick turns the bike the way it is pushed for as long as it is held, and a bike left leaned over with the stick centred comes back upright | j: p11-steering-that-turns-the-way-you-push
- [x] **V20** the drawn bike is not where the solver put it, and the rider is not on it. Reported by the user from the seat and visible in any --shot of a ride: the bike is buried in the dirt to about its seat with neither wheel meeting the ground, and the rider stands bolt upright above it with its hands well off the bars - after V18, which fixed which machine poses the body. The solver's own numbers say the placement is right, so this is the renderer: four seconds into a no-input ride the chassis is at y 336.02 with both wheels in contact, the front spring carrying 200 lbf and the rear 194 of the bike's 400, and the suspension at 0.45 of its 1.2 feet of travel - a bike sitting on its springs, not one sunk in the ground. Three things to compare and assert rather than eyeball: the drawn chassis origin against ride.position(); each drawn wheel's lowest point against the contact point the ground solver found; and the posed rider's hand and foot anchors against the chassis's, which crates/game-render/tests/body.rs already measures to a third of an inch in isolation and which the shell should reproduce on a real frame. Whichever of the three disagrees is the bug — crates/openmx/src/main.rs, crates/game-render/src/rider.rs and crates/game-render/src/chassis.rs | t: crates/game-render/tests/body.rs - a frame of the shell's own ride draws the wheels on the ground the solver found and the rider's hands on the bars | j: v20-drawn-where-the-solver-put-it
- [x] **V19** a wrecked rider never stops flailing. crates/game-sim/src/rider.rs says it in the doc comment on body_frame: rider_anim::Body::settled, the flag that decides whether a wrecked body flails or holds a limb still, is written by the wreck's own mode machine in the original, that machine is not ported, and so nothing in the port ever sets it - a body this port wrecks flails until a shell tells it it has settled, and no shell does. - [x] **P12** the stick is not connected to the front wheel. mv::steer_command (move.rs:680) works out max_steer_angle * (lean * steer_scale) every substep and nothing ever writes it to a wheel: ride.rs's patch reads wheel.w.steer_angle, whose only writer is wheel::steer_integrate, which wheel::integrate (wheel.rs:560-592) runs only for a balancing wheel and otherwise skips with a comment that the commanded angle stands - at zero, because nothing commanded it. Note that the committed stand-in .phy has no SteerScale*/SteerSpeed* keys, so a fixture needs them patched in (P10's ridden_bike does) or the scale loads as zero as well — crates/game-sim/src/ride.rs and crates/game-sim/src/wheel.rs | t: it_turns_the_way_the_stick_is_pushed in crates/game-sim/tests/ride.rs (ignored today) | j: the-stick-and-the-hinge
- [x] **P13** the brake cannot stop the bike: a locked clutch re-drives the rear wheel at the engine's idle. Ten seconds of full brake takes P10's fixture from 68 ft/s to 27.4 and holds it there for ever, which is the bike's road speed at MinRPM in first gear - mv::engine_update (move.rs:889-942) sets rpm = road_rpm with the clutch locked and then clamps it up into min_rpm..rev_limit, so under about 18 mph the engine drives the rear wheel against the brake. The engine sits at exactly MinRPM for the whole brake leg, measured. The one rule that would let the clutch out wants the speed under mv::IDLE_SPEED (7.33 ft/s), which the bike can never reach again. The human's own recording comes to a stop 39 times, so this is the port and not the bike. Decide what the original does with a locked clutch at walking pace (the idle floor is the engine's, not the gearbox's) and make P10's it_brakes_to_a_stop pass; take its #[ignore] off in both crates/game-sim/tests/ride.rs and crates/openmx/tests/ride.rs — crates/game-sim/src/move.rs and crates/game-sim/src/ride.rs | t: it_brakes_to_a_stop in crates/game-sim/tests/ride.rs (ignored today) | j: the-brake-that-never-stops
- [x] **P17** the rotating moves are hard-coded to the one the disc has. `Air::flip_torque` only ever writes x and only ever negative (crates/game-sim/src/air.rs:145), the trigger is one axis of one stick, and the 24 stunts rotate nothing — `trick.rs`'s head says so, and names `Trick::BACKFLIP`, `WHEELIE` and `STOPPIE` as separate ids driven by the air code's own torque parameters. The user wants frontflips, 360s and tricks out of other games later (2026-10-17), so give a rotation a shape that takes a new one as data: a trigger gesture, an axis, the torque and time pair, the decay rates and what gives control back, with the disc's backflip as one row reading exactly the `Params` keys it reads today. The port's default set stays exactly the disc's — the air tests and the acceptance run must not move a bit, which is the house rule for additions (AGENT.md: with them off the game behaves exactly like the original) — and anything invented sits behind a flag that is off by default, so a frontflip is a row someone adds rather than a patch to the step. - [x] **P16** you cannot pump a lip and flip off it with the controls the shell has. Both gestures are one stick axis crossed from one stop to the other while the lip holds: `on_a_lip` (crates/game-sim/src/air.rs:487) wants a wheel down, the chassis already rising faster than 5 ft/s, contact speed over 30 and no brake, and inside that window `backflip` (air.rs:552) arms below −0.8 on the stick's y and fires above +0.8, with `preload` (air.rs:502) the same cross the other way round. The shell's arrows are not a stick: `KeyAxis` takes 0.18 s to reach a stop and 0.10 s to come back (crates/openmx/src/pad.rs:252), so the cross costs about 0.22 s of lip, and the lip of a jump does not last that long. Measure how long `on_a_lip` is actually true over P10's jump leg, then decide what gives: a faster ramp for a reversal (the axis is already at a stop, so a reversal is not the slam `KeyAxis` exists to prevent), or keys bound to the d-pad — `keyboard()` (pad.rs:572) binds no Up/Down/Left/Right at all, so `Controls::dpad_y` and `dpad_dir` (crates/game-sim/src/ride.rs:142) are dead on a keyboard, which also puts the bike's own `Min/MaxDpadPreloadTime` pair (there precisely because a d-pad "cannot be held half way") out of reach and leaves the trick decoder on its stick fallback — or both. Nothing in `air.rs` may move: the gesture is the disc's and it is proved. Prove the fix as an eleventh leg of the minute — run-up, pump on the lip, flip, land on the wheels — in `crates/openmx/tests/ride-minute.pad`, asserted in both ride tests the way the other legs are → crates/openmx/src/pad.rs · crates/openmx/tests/ride-minute.pad · crates/openmx/tests/ride.rs · crates/game-sim/tests/ride.rs | t: it_flips_off_the_lip in crates/openmx/tests/ride.rs and crates/game-sim/tests/ride.rs | j: p16-a-flip-you-can-do — S31 measures the lip window on the original, off a human's backflips in recording2.p2m2 — take that number over P10's jump leg if it has landed
- [x] **P14** the camera does not follow the rider: the shell picks `cpresets.ini`'s first record and rides it all session. crates/openmx/src/main.rs:579 reads the track's presets in file order and main.rs:594 starts on index 0, which on free06 is `Race Cam 1` — the track ships eleven of them (`Race Cam 1`, `Race Cam 2`, `Freestyle Cam`, `Slot Cam`, the look-backs, the attract cams) plus a copy of each for every split-screen layout, per research/journal/2026-10-15-s7-s7-camera — and a freeride world is not a race, so the mode's own camera is the freestyle one. - [x] **P18** the shell draws 640x448 and blows it up. `Args` defaults to 640x448 at scale 1 (crates/openmx/src/main.rs:1273) and `Window::present` duplicates each frame pixel `scale` times across and down before it goes over the X11 socket (crates/openmx/src/window.rs:303), so play.sh's `--scale 2` is a 1280x896 window showing 640x448 pixels and every edge in the picture is two pixels wide. Draw at the window's own pixels instead: the setup parse keeps only root, root_visual and root_depth (window.rs:509) and throws the screen's two extents away, so read them, default the frame to the display's size, open the window at it, and read `ConfigureNotify` — nothing reads it today — so a resize re-sizes the frame rather than stretching one. `--size` and `--scale` stay, for the PS2-native 640x448 path and for the tests. Two things not to lose: the rasteriser is software, so 1920x1080 is 7.2x the pixels of 640x448 and the fps line (main.rs:1101) is the measurement that says whether the frame still fits in 60 Hz — report it at both sizes in the journal, and if it does not fit, say so with the number instead of lowering the frame clamp, which is the simulation's and is the game's; and the picture is 4:3, so a 16:9 window pillarboxes until V3 does widescreen for real — `vertical_fov` already takes the window's aspect and `Switches::short_viewport` (main.rs:1001) reads it too, so neither may be fed a stretched one → crates/openmx/src/window.rs · crates/openmx/src/main.rs | t: crates/openmx/src/window.rs's own tests, for the setup parse's screen size and for a resize; plus the fps numbers at both sizes in the journal | j: p18-native-resolution
- [x] **P15** the bike does not ride like MX Unleashed and nothing in the port says by how much. P10's minute asserts what a rider would notice — it goes, it holds a speed, it turns the way the stick is pushed, it lands on its wheels, it stops — but every threshold in it is the port's own invention and both files say so, and they ride `stock::BIKE` and `tests/freeride-bike.phy.txt`, whose heads say no number in them came off a disc. Put the original's numbers beside them for the bike the recording actually uses (D7: Race125, Race tier): top speed on the flat, the time to 30 and to 60, the turn rate at a held speed and a held stick, the air time and the distance off a lip, what a landing costs in speed, and how long a full brake takes from top speed. ### Phase 12 — riding together: split screen, menus, online
- [x] **R9** the original's two-player game has never been read. Find out from the disc how it splits the screen and runs two riders: the viewport layouts (the track's `cpresets.ini` has a copy of every camera preset per layout, `Preset1h`, `Preset1v`, `Preset1w` and `Preset1x`, picked by viewport index per research/journal/2026-10-15-s7-s7-camera — say what each of the four suffixes is, since four layouts for a two-player game suggests the engine knows more than two), where the second rider spawns in freeride and in a race, how pads are given to players, what the HUD draws per viewport, whether `Switches::short_viewport`'s pitch trim (crates/openmx/src/main.rs:1001) is the split screen's, what is cut back with two views up (draw distance, LOD, AI riders, effects), and how two riders touch (reason 7, `Joust`, in `air::Wreck`). Each view has its own camera following its rider, its own HUD, and `short_viewport` and `vertical_fov` fed that view's own aspect. The rasteriser is software, so measure: the fps line (crates/openmx/src/main.rs:1101) at 1, 2, 4 and 8 views at the window's size, in the journal, and if four do not fit in 60 Hz say so with the number rather than lowering anything the simulation reads. Make the aspect dynamic: the frame fills the window at any shape (16:9, 16:10, 21:9, a portrait window, a resize mid-ride) and each split-screen view (V22) takes its own viewport's aspect, so a 2-up or 4-up split is not squashed. ### Phase 13 — how it looks, sounds and plays
- [x] **V25** the world looks flat. Give it the original's textures and lighting — like the original, not 1:1 (rendering is by eye, AGENT.md). Today crates/game-render/src/raster.rs paints a texture times one per-vertex `light` and a fog blend, and models whose material has no resolved texture come out flat white (world.rs `textures: Vec<Option<usize>>`). Find every material that loses its texture and why (unresolved `.psm`/`.pst` names, multi-texture or detail layers, the terrain's blended layers, alpha-tested foliage and fences, the bike's and rider's liveries), and light the scene the way the track's files say: the scene's sun, ambient and fog, any baked vertex colours, the original's sky. Compare against the human's recordings' savestates (`./kit shot` on context/recordings' .p2s) side by side and write the differences down in the journal → crates/game-render/src/world.rs · crates/game-render/src/raster.rs · crates/game-render/src/terrain.rs | t: crates/game-render's own tests — a material that used to come out untextured now resolves, and a lit triangle's colour against a hand-worked one | j: textures-and-lighting

Also check that P35's virtual-pad filter catches what is plugged in on this machine, and say in the journal what it found → crates/openmx/src/menu.rs · crates/openmx/src/pad.rs · crates/openmx/src/main.rs | t: crates/openmx/tests/menu.rs — on the splash an idle pad's drift and a second pad's stick move nothing; the device that presses Cross or Enter is 1P and the only one driving the main menu; an unplug of 1P's device brings the splash back | j: press-start-splash

- [x] **N1** online play needs a whole state you can save and restore, and a simulation that is the same on every machine. Rollback saves the state every tick and replays from an old one when a late input arrives, so the ride (all of S54's players, the world's moving parts, the rng, the tricks, the wrecks, the camera's state if it reaches the simulation) must clone and restore cheaply and hash the same after. Take opentennis's way (`crates/hst-play/src/play/snapshot.rs`: `save`, `restore`, constants behind a shared `Arc` the hash skips, `encode`/`decode` for a resync), and extend crates/game-sim/tests/platforms.rs (it already compares across platforms) so x86_64, aarch64 and wasm32 agree on a multi-player ride's hashes. Prove save, step, restore, step gives the same hash, and run ggrs' SyncTest over it the way `HST_NET_SYNCTEST` does → crates/game-sim/src/ride.rs · crates/game-sim/src/lib.rs · crates/game-sim/tests/platforms.rs | t: crates/game-sim/tests/snapshot.rs — save/step/restore/step agrees, and a SyncTest over a scripted ride finds no mismatch | j: n1-save-and-restore — S54 landed; left: tests/players/mod.rs onto one Sim (add_player/step_all), re-pin RIDERS_* with print_the_riders_vectors, rerun tests/cross/platforms.sh
- [x] **P44** the shell runs very laggy, and it draws on one CPU thread in software (crates/game-render/src/raster.rs). Make GPU rendering the default: a wgpu renderer (opentennis uses wgpu through bevy — take what fits from there: research/journal/*-b273-renderer, *-b316-renderer-choices, *-b328-mobile-fps, *-p28-ios-gpu-time) that runs on desktop, in the browser (WebGPU with WebGL2 as fallback, so N4's web build draws through it rather than presenting a software frame) and later on phones. Rendering is not 1:1 with the original (AGENT.md): design it to be fast — batched draws per material, textures uploaded once, skinning on the GPU, culling, one pass per split view (V22) — and keep the software rasteriser only as a fallback setting (`--renderer gpu|software`). First measure, the way opentennis did (research/journal/*-z1-perf, *-b163-lag-spikes, *-p52-per-frame-clones, *-f0-uncapped-fps, *-b186-show-fps): an fps and slow-frame readout (`--fps`) and per-stage times (sim, animation, skinning, scene, draw, present) on one player and on four, before and after, in the journal. Also cut the CPU side's per-frame clones and allocations. Target: a steady 60 at 1080p with four views on this machine. The simulation must not move a bit → crates/game-render/src/gpu.rs · crates/game-render/src/world.rs · crates/openmx/src/main.rs · crates/openmx/src/window.rs | t: crates/game-render's tests — the GPU renderer draws a known scene headless (offscreen) and it matches the software one within a tolerance; the replay and acceptance runs unchanged | j: gpu-renderer

### Stretch
- [x] **K2** Port the kits merge fixer down into ps2kit/runner.py, replacing the long-lived tmux master. Upstream in ps2-decomp-kit: linux 50c040f (merge df47d27), windows 73b6e08 (merge a9dec98). `fix_merge()` is one `claude -p` per failed merge, synchronous, under the MERGE lock the runner already holds, prompted with the conflicted files, the branch and the last 12 lines of notes/master.md. That deletes everything here that exists only to keep a pane alive: OUTBOX, LOCK, MARK, master_idle/master_busy, the 90-minute stale lock, the starvation bypass, pane adoption, and `enter()` — whose bracketed-paste-and-count-the-Enters delivery is the fragile part of the current design and the reason the one-shot is worth having. It also works with no tmux, which today degrades to LEFT FOR A HUMAN. New config: `[run] fix_merges` (default true), `fix_timeout` (1200s, then the merge is left for a human), `fix_model` (empty = KIT_MODEL or opus), and `[git] push_merge`; `fix_cmd()` sits beside `agent_cmd()` and honours KIT_AGENT and KIT_LEAN the same way. **Set `push_merge = false`** — it defaults true upstream, and openmx deliberately does not publish the shared branch (AGENT.md, PLAN.md and the master prompt all say so; 008f58e). kit.toml is untracked here, so that key is a hand edit to the local file, and not while a run is going. The fixer does not run the tests: the runner runs the gate itself once the fixer commits. Verify with upstreams `test_runner_fixer` in tests/test_plan.py, which drives a real two-sided conflict through a stub agent, plus ./kit check → ps2kit/runner.py | t: tests/test_plan.py | j: k2-port-merge-fixer — two corrections found while checking repos-c3s claim that the fixer is unreachable on the sequential path. It is unreachable here too, and for the same reason: openmx `sequential()` runs in the main checkout on the current branch, commits straight onto it, and never calls `merge()` or `finish()`, so there is no conflict for a fixer to reach and the port needs no `has_master`-style flag. Which means this task line was wrong to say the port buys a no-tmux path: there is nothing to degrade from. Second, `finish()`s `has_master=False` branch (the LEFT FOR A HUMAN log) has no caller — both call sites are on the parallel path and pass four args — so it is dead code; the docstring that described it is fixed, and the port should drop the parameter rather than carry it down
- [x] **K3** the (after X) gate reads only single-id dependencies: the runner's regex wants one run of non-space characters before the closing bracket, so a line gated on a list — (after S17, S18, S19) — matches nothing and the task starts whenever its files are free, which is how the shell rewire began an hour before the controllers it needs were in the branch; make the gate read every id in the list, and say in the module docstring which forms it accepts
